{"parentUuid":"98e2ac15-fa89-4058-872c-02cda97dd7d1","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a74ced13d3fb354be","attachment":{"type":"skill_listing","content":"- heartbeat-protocol: Claude node contract for DebateAI Graph Spine v2. The Main Orchestrator (Claude-Router seat) contract; thin loader over the repo spine. All agent launches use /goal.\n- commit-commands:clean_gone: Cleans up all git branches marked as [gone] (branches that have been deleted on the remote but still exist locally), including removing associated worktrees.\n- commit-commands:commit-push-pr: Commit, push, and open a PR\n- commit-commands:commit: Create a git commit\n- pdf-viewer:annotate: Collaboratively annotate a PDF — propose markup, review together, iterate\n- pdf-viewer:fill-form: Fill PDF form fields interactively with live visual feedback\n- pdf-viewer:open: Open a PDF in the interactive viewer\n- pdf-viewer:sign: Place a signature or initials image on a PDF\n- frontend-design:frontend-design: Guidance for distinctive, intentional visual design when building new UI or reshaping an existing one. Helps with aesthetic direction, typography, and making choices that don't read as templated defaults.\n- mattpocock-skills:diagnosing-bugs: Diagnosis loop for hard bugs and performance regressions. Use when the user says \"diagnose\"/\"debug this\", or reports something broken/throwing/failing/slow.\n- mattpocock-skills:tdd: Test-driven development. Use when the user wants to build features or fix bugs test-first, mentions \"red-green-refactor\", or wants integration tests.\n- mattpocock-skills:prototype: Build a throwaway prototype to answer a design question. Use when the user wants to sanity-check whether a state model or logic feels right, or explore what a UI should look like.\n- mattpocock-skills:research: Investigate a question against high-trust primary sources and capture the findings as a Markdown file in the repo. Use when the user wants a topic researched, docs or API facts gathered, or reading legwork delegated to a background agent.\n- mattpocock-skills:domain-modeling: Build and sharpen a project's domain model. Use when the user wants to pin down domain terminology or a ubiquitous language, record an architectural decision, or when another skill needs to maintain the domain model.\n- mattpocock-skills:codebase-design: Shared vocabulary for designing deep modules. Use when the user wants to design or improve a module's interface, find deepening opportunities, decide where a seam goes, make code more testable or AI-navigable, or when another skill needs the deep-module vocabulary.\n- mattpocock-skills:code-review: Review the changes since a fixed point (commit, branch, tag, or merge-base) along two axes — Standards (does the code follow this repo's documented coding standards?) and Spec (does the code match what the originating issue/PRD asked for?). Runs both reviews in parallel sub-agents and reports them side by side. Use when the user wants to review a branch, a PR, work-in-progress changes, or asks to \"review since X\".\n- mattpocock-skills:resolving-merge-conflicts: Use when you need to resolve an in-progress git merge/rebase conflict.\n- mattpocock-skills:grilling: Grill the user relentlessly about a plan, decision, or idea. Use when the user wants to stress-test their thinking, or uses any 'grill' trigger phrases.\n- superpowers:brainstorming: You MUST use this before any creative work - creating features, building components, adding functionality, or modifying behavior. Explores user intent, requirements and design before implementation.\n- superpowers:dispatching-parallel-agents: Use when facing 2+ independent tasks that can be worked on without shared state or sequential dependencies\n- superpowers:executing-plans: Use when you have a written implementation plan to execute in a separate session with review checkpoints\n- superpowers:finishing-a-development-branch: Use when implementation is complete, all tests pass, and you need to decide how to integrate the work\n- superpowers:receiving-code-review: Use when receiving code review feedback, before implementing suggestions, especially if feedback seems unclear or technically questionable - requires technical rigor and verification, not performative agreement or blind implementation\n- superpowers:requesting-code-review: Use when completing tasks, implementing major features, or before merging to verify work meets requirements\n- superpowers:subagent-driven-development: Use when executing implementation plans with independent tasks in the current session\n- superpowers:systematic-debugging: Use when encountering any bug, test failure, or unexpected behavior, before proposing fixes\n- superpowers:test-driven-development: Use when implementing any feature or bugfix, before writing implementation code\n- superpowers:using-git-worktrees: Use when starting feature work that needs isolation from current workspace or before executing implementation plans - ensures an isolated workspace exists via native tools or git worktree fallback\n- superpowers:using-superpowers: Use when starting any conversation - establishes how to find and use skills, requiring skill invocation before ANY response including clarifying questions\n- superpowers:verification-before-completion: Use when about to claim work is complete, fixed, or passing, before committing or creating PRs - requires running verification commands and confirming output before making any success claims; evidence before assertions always\n- superpowers:writing-plans: Use when you have a spec or requirements for a multi-step task, before touching code\n- superpowers:writing-skills: Use when creating new skills, editing existing skills, or verifying skills work before deployment\n- pdf-viewer:view-pdf: Interactive PDF viewer. Use when the user wants to open, show, or view a PDF and collaborate on it visually — annotate, highlight, stamp, fill form fields, place signature/initials, or review markup together. Not for summarization or text extraction (use native Read instead).\n- productivity:memory-management: Two-tier memory system that makes Claude a true workplace collaborator. Decodes shorthand, acronyms, nicknames, and internal language so Claude understands requests like a colleague would. CLAUDE.md for working memory, memory/ directory for the full knowledge base.\n- productivity:start: Initialize the productivity system and open the dashboard. Use when setting up the plugin for the first time, bootstrapping working memory from your existing task list, or decoding the shorthand (nicknames, acronyms, project codenames) you use in your todos.\n- productivity:task-management: Simple task management using a shared TASKS.md file. Reference this when the user asks about their tasks, wants to add/complete tasks, or needs help tracking commitments.\n- productivity:update: Sync tasks and refresh memory from your current activity. Use when pulling new assignments from your project tracker into TASKS.md, triaging stale or overdue tasks, filling memory gaps for unknown people or projects, or running a comprehensive scan to catch todos buried in chat and email.\n- cowork-plugin-management:cowork-plugin-customizer: Customize a Claude Code plugin for a specific organization's tools and workflows. Use when: customize plugin, set up plugin, configure plugin, tailor plugin, adjust plugin settings, customize plugin connectors, customize plugin skill, tweak plugin, modify plugin configuration.\n- cowork-plugin-management:create-cowork-plugin: Guide users through creating a new plugin from scratch in a cowork session. Use when users want to create a plugin, build a plugin, make a new plugin, develop a plugin, scaffold a plugin, start a plugin from scratch, or design a plugin. This skill requires Cowork mode with access to the outputs directory for delivering the final .plugin file.\n- engineering:architecture: Create or evaluate an architecture decision record (ADR). Use when choosing between technologies (e.g., Kafka vs SQS), documenting a design decision with trade-offs and consequences, reviewing a system design proposal, or designing a new component from requirements and constraints.\n- engineering:code-review: Review code changes for security, performance, and correctness. Trigger with a PR URL or diff, \"review this before I merge\", \"is this code safe?\", or when checking a change for N+1 queries, injection risks, missing edge cases, or error handling gaps.\n- engineering:debug: Structured debugging session — reproduce, isolate, diagnose, and fix. Trigger with an error message or stack trace, \"this works in staging but not prod\", \"something broke after the deploy\", or when behavior diverges from expected and the cause isn't obvious.\n- engineering:deploy-checklist: Pre-deployment verification checklist. Use when about to ship a release, deploying a change with database migrations or feature flags, verifying CI status and approvals before going to production, or documenting rollback triggers ahead of time.\n- engineering:documentation: Write and maintain technical documentation. Trigger with \"write docs for\", \"document this\", \"create a README\", \"write a runbook\", \"onboarding guide\", or when the user needs help with any form of technical writing — API docs, architecture docs, or operational runbooks.\n- engineering:incident-response: Run an incident response workflow — triage, communicate, and write postmortem. Trigger with \"we have an incident\", \"production is down\", an alert that needs severity assessment, a status update mid-incident, or when writing a blameless postmortem after resolution.\n- engineering:standup: Generate a standup update from recent activity. Use when preparing for daily standup, summarizing yesterday's commits and PRs and ticket moves, formatting work into yesterday/today/blockers, or structuring a few rough notes into a shareable update.\n- engineering:system-design: Design systems, services, and architectures. Trigger with \"design a system for\", \"how should we architect\", \"system design for\", \"what's the right architecture for\", or when the user needs help with API design, data modeling, or service boundaries.\n- engineering:tech-debt: Identify, categorize, and prioritize technical debt. Trigger with \"tech debt\", \"technical debt audit\", \"what should we refactor\", \"code health\", or when the user asks about code quality, refactoring priorities, or maintenance backlog.\n- engineering:testing-strategy: Design test strategies and test plans. Trigger with \"how should we test\", \"test strategy for\", \"write tests for\", \"test plan\", \"what tests do we need\", or when the user needs help with testing approaches, coverage, or test architecture.\n- design:accessibility-review: Run a WCAG 2.1 AA accessibility audit on a design or page. Trigger with \"audit accessibility\", \"check a11y\", \"is this accessible?\", or when reviewing a design for color contrast, keyboard navigation, touch target size, or screen reader behavior before handoff.\n- design:design-critique: Get structured design feedback on usability, hierarchy, and consistency. Trigger with \"review this design\", \"critique this mockup\", \"what do you think of this screen?\", or when sharing a Figma link or screenshot for feedback at any stage from exploration to final polish.\n- design:design-handoff: Generate developer handoff specs from a design. Use when a design is ready for engineering and needs a spec sheet covering layout, design tokens, component props, interaction states, responsive breakpoints, edge cases, and animation details.\n- design:design-system: Audit, document, or extend your design system. Use when checking for naming inconsistencies or hardcoded values across components, writing documentation for a component's variants, states, and accessibility notes, or designing a new pattern that fits the existing system.\n- design:research-synthesis: Synthesize user research into themes, insights, and recommendations. Use when you have interview transcripts, survey results, usability test notes, support tickets, or NPS responses that need to be distilled into patterns, user segments, and prioritized next steps.\n- design:user-research: Plan, conduct, and synthesize user research. Trigger with \"user research plan\", \"interview guide\", \"usability test\", \"survey design\", \"research questions\", or when the user needs help with any aspect of understanding their users through research.\n- design:ux-copy: Write or review UX copy — microcopy, error messages, empty states, CTAs. Trigger with \"write copy for\", \"what should this button say?\", \"review this error message\", or when naming a CTA, wording a confirmation dialog, filling an empty state, or writing onboarding text.\n- anthropic-skills:consolidate-memory: Reflective pass over your memory files — merge duplicates, fix stale facts, prune the index.\n- anthropic-skills:docx: Use this skill whenever the user wants to create, read, edit, or manipulate Word documents (.docx files) or Word templates (.dotx files). Triggers include: any mention of 'Word doc', 'word document', '.docx', '.dotx', or requests to produce professional documents with formatting like tables of contents, headings, page numbers, or letterheads. Also use when extracting or reorganizing content from .docx or .dotx files, inserting or replacing images in documents, performing find-and-replace in Word files, working with tracked changes or comments, or converting content into a polished Word document. If the user asks for a 'report', 'memo', 'letter', 'template', or similar deliverable as a Word or .docx file, use this skill. Do NOT use for PDFs, spreadsheets, Google Docs, or general coding tasks unrelated to document generation.\n- anthropic-skills:explain-usage: Explain where this session's tokens went, with one simple chart in plain language. Use when the user says things like \"explain my usage\", \"where did my tokens go\", or asks for a usage breakdown.\n- anthropic-skills:morning: Render the user's morning brief as a styled HTML artifact, or set it up as a recurring weekday task. Use only when the user explicitly asks to run, see, or set up their morning brief, or if they invoke /morning by name. A question about their day, schedule, or calendar is not by itself a request for the brief; answer it directly instead.\n- anthropic-skills:pdf: Use this skill whenever the user wants to do anything with PDF files. This includes reading or extracting text/tables from PDFs, combining or merging multiple PDFs into one, splitting PDFs apart, rotating pages, adding watermarks, creating new PDFs, filling PDF forms, encrypting/decrypting PDFs, extracting images, and OCR on scanned PDFs to make them searchable. If the user mentions a .pdf file or asks to produce one, use this skill.\n- anthropic-skills:pptx: Use this skill any time a .pptx or .potx file is involved in any way — as input, output, or both. This includes: creating slide decks, pitch decks, or presentations; reading, parsing, or extracting text from any .pptx or .potx file (even if the extracted content will be used elsewhere, like in an email or summary); editing, modifying, or updating existing presentations; combining or splitting slide files; working with templates (.potx), layouts, speaker notes, or comments. Trigger whenever the user mentions \"deck,\" \"slides,\" \"presentation,\" or references a .pptx or .potx filename, regardless of what they plan to do with the content afterward. If a .pptx or .potx file needs to be opened, created, or touched, use this skill.\n- anthropic-skills:schedule: Create or update a scheduled task that runs automatically. Use when the user says things like \"every day\", \"each morning\", \"remind me in an hour\", \"run this at noon\", or wants to reschedule an existing task.\n- anthropic-skills:setup-cowork: Guided Cowork setup — install role-matched plugins, connect your tools, try a skill.\n- anthropic-skills:skill-creator: Create new skills, modify and improve existing skills, and measure skill performance. Use when users want to create a skill from scratch, edit, or optimize an existing skill, run evals to test a skill, benchmark skill performance with variance analysis, or optimize a skill's description for better triggering accuracy.\n- anthropic-skills:xlsx: Use this skill any time a spreadsheet file is the primary input or output. This means any task where the user wants to: open, read, edit, or fix an existing .xlsx, .xlsm, .xltx, .csv, or .tsv file (e.g., adding columns, computing formulas, formatting, charting, cleaning messy data); create a new spreadsheet from scratch or from other data sources; or convert between tabular file formats. Trigger especially when the user references a spreadsheet file by name or path — even casually (like \"the xlsx in my downloads\") — and wants something done to it or produced from it. Also trigger for cleaning or restructuring messy tabular data files (malformed rows, misplaced headers, junk data) into proper spreadsheets. The deliverable must be a spreadsheet file. Do NOT trigger when the primary deliverable is a Word document, HTML report, standalone Python script, database pipeline, or Google Sheets API integration, even if tabular data is involved.\n- dataviz: Use this skill whenever you are about to create ANY chart, graph, plot, dashboard, or data visualization, in ANY output medium — an HTML or React artifact, inline SVG, plotting code in any library (matplotlib, plotly, d3, Recharts, …), an image/PNG you will render and upload, or a chart shared into Slack. Read it BEFORE writing the first line of chart code, choosing chart colors, building a stat tile / meter / KPI row, or laying out a dashboard. Produces visualizations that read as one system — elegant, accessible, consistent in light and dark — using a brand-neutral placeholder palette you swap for your own. Teaches a design-system-agnostic method: a form heuristic, a color formula with a runnable validator, mark specs, and interaction rules. A validated default palette is documented in `references/palette.md` — swap that file's values for your brand's. Triggers on: \"chart\", \"graph\", \"plot\", \"data viz\", \"visualization\", \"dashboard\", \"analytics\", \"visualize data\", \"categorical colors\", \"sequential / diverging palette\", \"stat tile\", \"sparkline\", \"heatmap\", \"legend\", \"axis\", \"tooltip\", \"chart colors\", \"color by series\".\n- artifact-design: Design guidance and fundamentals for Artifacts.\n- artifact-diagramming: Diagramming know-how for Artifacts — when a picture earns its place, how to draw one that shows the real mechanism, and the inline-SVG mechanics that keep it legible in both themes.\n- artifact-capabilities: Runtime capabilities a published Artifact page can be granted — behavior static HTML cannot provide on its own, such as the page reading live or connected data, keeping state shared across viewers, or updating and republishing itself. Serves this user's live capability roster and the typed call definitions. Load it whenever the user asks for an artifact needing any such runtime behavior.\n- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review the changed code for reuse, simplification, efficiency, and altitude cleanups, then apply the fixes. Quality only — it does not hunt for bugs; use /code-review for that.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled cloud agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring cloud agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Reference for the Claude API / Anthropic SDK — model ids, pricing, params, streaming, tool use, MCP, agents, caching, token counting, model migration.\nTRIGGER — read BEFORE opening the target file; don't skip because it \"looks like a one-liner\" — whenever: the prompt names Claude/Anthropic in any form (Claude, Anthropic, Fable, Opus, Sonnet, Haiku, `anthropic`, `@anthropic-ai`, `claude-*`, `us.anthropic.*`, `[1m]`); the user asks about an LLM (pricing/model choice/limits/caching) — never answer from memory; OR the task is LLM-shaped with provider unstated (agent/MCP/tool-definition/multi-agent/RAG/LLM-judge/computer-use; generate/summarize/extract/classify/rewrite/converse over NL; debugging refusals/cutoffs/streaming/tool-calls/tokens).\nSKIP only when another provider is being worked on (overrides all triggers): OpenAI/GPT/Gemini/Llama/Mistral/Cohere/Ollama named in the query; OR `grep -rE 'openai|langchain_openai|google.generativeai|genai|mistralai|cohere|ollama'` over the project hits (run this grep FIRST if no provider named — don't Read the file).\n- run: Launch and drive this project's app to see a change working. Use when asked to run, start, or screenshot the app, or to confirm a change works in the real app (not just tests). First looks for a project skill that already covers launching the app; otherwise falls back to built-in patterns per project type (CLI, server, TUI, Electron, browser-driven, library).\n- init: disagreementFlags.filter(Boolean).length / disagreementFlags.length;\n290\t  return Object.freeze({\n291\t    observedRate,\n292\t    declaredPrice,\n293\t    absoluteDifference: Math.abs(observedRate - declaredPrice),\n294\t    n: disagreementFlags.length,\n295\t    basis: \"MEASURED_PROCESS\"\n296\t  });\n297\t}\n298\t\n299\texport interface RoutingDecision {\n300\t  readonly kind: \"FALLBACK\" | readonly guardTrail: readonly { readonly guard: RoutingGuard; readonly outcome: \"PASS\" | \"BYPASS\" | \"FALLBACK\" }[];\n305\t  readonly policy: { readonly rowKey: string; readonly registerVersion: number; readonly sourceRef: string };\n306\t}\n307\t\n308\tfunction intervalsOverlap(left: RoutingCandidate[\"interval\"], right: RoutingCandidate[\"interval\"]): boolean {\n309\t  return left.lower <= right.upper && right.lower <= left.upper;\n310\t}\n311\t\n312\texport function routeServedLane(input: {\n313\t  readonly taskClass: string;\n314\t  readonly lane: \"SERVED\" | \"PANEL\" | \"CRITIC\";\n315\t  readonly candidates: readonly RoutingCandidate[];\n316\t  readonly fallbackModelId: string;\n317\t  readonly explorationDraw: number;\n318\t  readonly policy: RoutingPolicy;\n319\t  readonly scorerModelId?: string;\n320\t}): RoutingDecision {\n321\t  for (const [name, value] of Object.entries({\n322\t    taskClass: input.taskClass,\n323\t    fallbackModelId: input.fallbackModelId,\n324\t    rowKey: input.policy.rowKey,\n325\t    sourceRef: input.policy.sourceRef\n326\t  })) assertNonBlank(value, name);\n327\t  assertUnitInterval(input.explorationDraw, \"exploration draw\");\n328\t  if (input.policy.explorationShare <= 0 || input.policy.explorationShare >= 1) {\n329\t    throw new TypedDomainError(\"EXPLORATION_FLOOR_INVALID\", \"The register-supplied exploration share must be non-zero and below one\");\n330\t  }\n331\t  if (input.scorerModelId !== undefined && input.candidates.some((candidate) => candidate.modelId === ROUTING_GUARDS.map((guard) => ({ guard, outcome: \"PASS\" as const }));\n336\t  const decision = (\n337\t    kind: RoutingDecision[\"kind\"],\n338\t    selectedModelId: string,\n339\t    propensity: number,\n340\t    outcomes: RoutingDecision[\"guardTrail\"] = trail\n341\t  ): RoutingDecision => Object.freeze({\n342\t    kind,\n343\t    selectedModelId,\n344\t    propensity,\n345\t    taskClass: input.taskClass,\n346\t    guardTrail: Object.freeze(outcomes.map((entry) => Object.freeze(entry))),\n347\t    policy\n348\t  });\n349\t  if (input.lane === \"PANEL\") return decision(\"CRITIC_EXEMPT\", input.fallbackModelId, 1, trail.map((entry) => ({ ...entry, outcome: entry.guard === \"G6\" ? \"BYPASS\" : entry.outcome })));\n351\t  const eligible = input.candidates\n352\t    .filter((candidate) => candidate.n >= input.policy.departNeutralMinimumN)\n353\t    .sort((left, right) => right.value - left.value || left.modelId.localeCompare(right.modelId));\n354\t  if (eligible.length < 2 || eligible.some((candidate) => candidate.n < input.policy.hardRoutingMinimumN)) {\n355\t    return decision(\"FALLBACK\", input.fallbackModelId, 1, trail.map((entry) => ({ ...entry, outcome: entry.guard === \"G4\" ? \"FALLBACK\" : entry.outcome })));\n356\t  }\n357\t  const [best, next] = eligible;\n358\t  if (best === undefined || next === undefined || intervalsOverlap(best.interval, next.interval)) {\n359\t    return decision(\"FALLBACK\", input.fallbackModelId, 1, trail.map((entry) => ({ ...entry, outcome: entry.guard === \"G4\" || entry.guard === \"G5\" ? \"FALLBACK\" : entry.outcome })));\n360\t  }\n361\t  return input.explorationDraw < input.policy.explorationShare\n362\t    ? DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),\n25\t  outcome_attempt_id uuid NOT NULL UNIQUE,\n26\t  answer_id uuid NOT NULL,\n27\t  answer_version integer NOT NULL CHECK (answer_version > 0),\n28\t  as_of timestamptz 0),\n37\t  resolver_ref text NOT NULL CHECK (length(btrim(resolver_ref)) > 0),\n38\t  resolver_is_external boolean NOT NULL CHECK (resolver_is_external),\n39\t  resolved_outcome boolean NOT 'PERMANENTLY_UNSCOREABLE', 'DISPUTED')),\n43\t  accepted boolean NOT NULL,\n44\t  superseded_by_answer_outcome_id uuid REFERENCES scorecard.answer_outcome(answer_outcome_id),\n45\t  proper_score_total type { Pool } from \"pg\";\n2\timport { evaluate, type OperatorResolution } from \"@debateai/propagation\";\n3\timport { decideReplayEviction, ServeRepository } from \"@debateai/serve\";\n4\timport { LivenessRepository } from \"@debateai/liveness\";\n5\timport { readLivenessPolicy } from \"@debateai/register\";\n6\timport {\n7\t  SettlementRepository,\n8\t  type SettlementOutcomeInput,\n9\t  type SettlementPolicy,\n10\t  type SettlementResult\n11\t} from \"@debateai/settlement\";\n12\t\n13\texport interface ReplaySelfTestReport {\n14\t  readonly checked: number;\n15\t  readonly evicted: readonly string[];\n16\t}\n17\t\n18\texport async function runReplaySelfTest(pool: Pool): Promise<ReplaySelfTestReport> {\n19\t  const records = await pool.query<{\n20\t    served_number_id: string;\n21\t    stored: number;\n22\t    node_id: string;\n23\t    tau: number;\n24\t    arrow_order: readonly string[];\n25\t    cluster_records: readonly unknown[];\n26\t    operator_by_parent: readonly OperatorResolution[];\n27\t  }>(\n28\t    `SELECT number.served_number_id, number.value AS stored,\n29\t            strength.node_id, judgement.tau, propagation.arrow_order,\n30\t            propagation.cluster_records, propagation.operator_by_parent\n31\t     FROM serve.served_number AS number\n32\t     JOIN serve.served_number_event AS latest\n33\t       ON latest.served_number_id = number.served_number_id\n34\t     JOIN ledger.propagation_run AS propagation\n35\t       ON propagation.propagation_run_id = number.provenance_ref\n36\t     JOIN ledger.node_strength_record AS strength\n37\t       ON strength.propagation_run_id = propagation.propagation_run_id\n38\t     JOIN LATERAL (\n39\t       SELECT tau FROM ledger.reduced_judgement\n40\t       WHERE node_id = strength.node_id ORDER BY at_seq DESC LIMIT 1\n41\t     ) AS judgement ON true\n42\t     WHERE latest.at_seq = (\n43\t       SELECT max(event.at_seq) FROM serve.served_number_event AS event\n44\t       WHERE event.served_number_id = number.served_number_id\n45\t     ) AND latest.status = 'PRESENT'\n46\t     ORDER BY number.served_number_id`\n47\t  );\n48\t  const evicted: string[] = [];\n49\t  const serve = new ServeRepository(pool);\n50\t  for (const row of records.rows) {\n51\t    if (row.arrow_order.length > 0) {\n52\t      throw new TypeError(\"CONTINUOUS_REPLAY_SHAPE_NOT_IMPLEMENTED\");\n53\t    }\n54\t    const outcome = evaluate({\n55\t      nodes: [{ nodeId: row.node_id, baseStrength: Number(row.tau) }],\n56\t      arrows: [],\n57\t      arrowOrder: row.arrow_order,\n58\t      operatorResolutions: row.operator_by_parent,\n59\t      clusterRecords: row.cluster_records\n60\t    });\n61\t    const decision = decideReplayEviction({\n62\t      stored: Number(row.stored),\n63\t      recomputed: outcome.strengths[0]!.strength,\n64\t      servedNumberId: row.served_number_id\n65\t    });\n66\t    if (decision.kind === \"EVICT\") {\n67\t      await serve.recordReplayEviction(decision.servedNumberId);\n68\t      evicted.push(decision.servedNumberId);\n69\t    }\n70\t  }\n71\t  return { checked: records.rows.length, evicted };\n72\t}\n73\t\n74\texport async function runLivenessSweep(pool: Pool, now = new Date()): Promise<readonly string[]> {\n75\t  const versions = await pool.query<{ register_version: string }>(\n76\t    `SELECT DISTINCT register_version::text FROM core.run ORDER BY register_version`\n77\t  );\n78\t  const archived: string[] = [];\n79\t  const liveness = new LivenessRepository(pool);\n80\t  for (const row of versions.rows) {\n81\t    const policy = await readLivenessPolicy(pool, Number(row.register_version), \"standard\");\n82\t    archived.push(...await liveness.sweep(now, policy));\n83\t  }\n84\t  return Object.freeze(archived);\n85\t}\n86\t\n87\texport async function runReaper(_pool: Pool): Promise<never> {\n88\t  throw new Error(\"S00_SCAFFOLD_ONLY: job:reaper implementation belongs to its later slice\");\n89\t}\n90\t\n91\texport interface SettlementWatchReport {\n92\t  readonly checked: number;\n93\t  readonly settled: number;\n94\t  readonly superseded: number;\n95\t  readonly incomplete: number;\n96\t  readonly results: readonly SettlementResult[];\n97\t}\n98\t\n99\t// The resolver adapter supplies immutable outcome envelopes. Keeping that seam\n100\t// explicit prevents the scheduler from manufacturing an outcome or policy.\n101\texport async function runSettlementWatch(\n102\t  pool: Pool,\n103\t  outcomes: readonly SettlementOutcomeInput[] = [],\n104\t  policy?: SettlementPolicy\n105\t): Promise<SettlementWatchReport> {\n106\t  if (outcomes.length > 0 && policy === undefined) {\n107\t    throw new TypeError(\"SETTLEMENT_POLICY_REQUIRED: resolver outcomes require a registered scoring policy\");\n108\t  }\n109\t  const repository = new SettlementRepository(pool);\n110\t  const results: SettlementResult[] = [];\n111\t  for (const outcome of outcomes) results.push(await repository.settle(outcome, policy!));\n112\t  return Object.freeze({\n113\t    checked: outcomes.length,\n114\t    settled: results.filter((result) => result.kind === \"SETTLED\").length,\n115\t    superseded: results.filter((result) => result.kind === \"SUPERSEDED_ATTEMPT\").length,\n116\t    incomplete: results.filter((result) => result.kind === \"INCOMPLETE_RUN_SKIPPED\").length,\n117\t    results: Object.freeze(results)\n118\t  });\n119\t}\n120\t"}]},"uuid":"600c90bd-81b4-430a-8030-ee487d211b2c","timestamp":"2026-08-08T20:41:06.080Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"a053cb13-d73d-49f4-989b-de17f488e96a","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/vladmihaimiron/Documents/DebateAI-V3","sessionId":"a8fcf2e3-bdd1-4a10-bcd6-3ec887012907","version":"2.1.221","gitBranch":"main","slug":"hidden-riding-bengio"}

{ readFile, readdir } from \"node:fs/promises\";\n2\timport { AsyncLocalStorage } from \"node:async_hooks\";\n3\timport type { Pool, PoolClient } from \"pg\";\n4\timport pg from \"pg\";\n5\timport { TypedDomainError, type ActivationState, type CompositionBudgetTier, type RiskTier, type TierSource } from \"@debateai/kernel\";\n6\t\n7\tconst { Pool: PgPool } = pg;\n8\tconst writeTransaction = new AsyncLocalStorage<boolean>();\n9\t\n10\texport function createPool(connectionString: string): Pool {\n11\t  return new PgPool({ connectionString });\n12\t}\n13\t\n14\texport async function migrate(pool: Pool): Promise<void> {\n15\t  const directory = new URL(\"../../../migrations/\", import.meta.url);\n16\t  const migrations = (await readdir(directory)).filter((name) => /^\\d+.*\\.sql$/.test(name)).sort();\n17\t  const client = await pool.connect();\n18\t  try {\n19\t    await client.query(\"BEGIN\");\n20\t    await client.query(\"SELECT pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtextextended('debateai:schema-migrations', 0))\");\n21\t    await client.query(`\n22\t      CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS public.debateai_schema_migration (\n23\t        name text PRIMARY KEY CHECK (length(btrim(name)) > 0),\n24\t        applied_at timestamptz NOT NULL\n25\t      )\n26\t    `);\n27\t    for (const name of migrations) {\n28\t      const applied = await client.query(\"SELECT 1 FROM public.debateai_schema_migration WHERE name=$1\", [name]);\n29\t      if (applied.rowCount !== 0) continue;\n30\t      await client.query(await readFile(new URL(name, directory), \"utf8\"));\n31\t      await client.query(\n32\t        \"INSERT INTO public.debateai_schema_migration (name, applied_at) VALUES ($1, statement_timestamp())\",\n33\t        [name]\n34\t      );\n35\t    }\n36\t    await client.query(\"COMMIT\");\n37\t  } catch (error) {\n38\t    await client.query(\"ROLLBACK\");\n39\t    throw error;\n40\t  } finally {\n41\t    client.release();\n42\t  }\n43\t}\n44\t\n45\texport async function withWriteTransaction<T>(\n46\t  pool: Pool,\n47\t  operation: (client: PoolClient) => Promise<T>\n48\t): Promise<T> {\n49\t  const client = await pool.connect();\n50\t  try {\n51\t    await client.query(\"BEGIN\");\n52\t    const result = await writeTransaction.run(true, () => operation(client));\n53\t    await client.query(\"COMMIT\");\n54\t    return result;\n55\t  } catch (error) {\n56\t    await client.query(\"ROLLBACK\");\n57\t    throw error;\n58\t  } finally {\n59\t    client.release();\n60\t  }\n61\t}\n62\t\n63\texport function assertNoOpenWriteTransaction(): void {\n64\t  if (writeTransaction.getStore() === true) {\n65\t    throw new TypedDomainError(\"PROVIDER_CALL_INSIDE_TRANSACTION\", \"A provider call cannot run inside a write transaction\");\n66\t  }\n67\t}\n68\t\n69\texport async function allocateSequence(client: PoolClient): Promise<number> {\n70\t  const result = await client.query<{ sequence: string }>(\"SELECT ledger.allocate_sequence() AS sequence\");\n71\t  const value = result.rows[0]?.sequence;\n72\t  if (value === undefined) throw new TypedDomainError(\"SEQUENCE_ALLOCATION_FAILED\", \"No sequence was allocated\");\n73\t  return Number(value);\n74\t}\n75\t\n76\texport interface InitialBatteryRow {\n77\t  readonly batteryRowId: string;\n78\t  readonly predicateRef: string;\n79\t  readonly openingState: ActivationState;\n80\t  readonly predicateInputs: Readonly<Record<string, unknown>>;\n81\t  readonly skipEvidence: Readonly<Record<string, unknown>> | null;\n82\t}\n83\t\n84\texport interface StartRunInput {\n85\t  readonly questionLine: string;\n86\t  readonly askerId: string;\n87\t  readonly sessionId: string;\n88\t  readonly callerScope: \"ASKER\" | \"OPERATOR\";\n89\t {"parentUuid":"6a3503a3-dd92-41da-84e4-8f2c2d0cd526","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a74ced13d3fb354be","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-8","id":"msg_011Cdqvuv47CP9TgK9QJb1Xd","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"thinking","thinking":"The integration test validates the first-settled-wins behavior where the winner gets marked SETTLED and the loser SUPERSEDED_ATTEMPT, with the loser recording accepted=false and a superseded_by pointer. It also checks that the scorecard freezes at one count, the budget remains unchanged in terms of model calls, the ledger captures all four action types, and that append-only constraints prevent updates to answer outcomes. There's also a check for incomplete run handling.\n\nThe key insight is that both settle calls operate on the same base (same answerId, answerVersion, asOf) but with different resolvedOutcome values—the winner has true, the loser has false. The winner inserts accepted=true as the first winner, while the loser finds that prior winner and inserts accepted=false with a superseded_by reference. Importantly, the scorecard only shows one cell because the loser returns early at SUPERSEDED_ATTEMPT before scorecard derivation even runs, so it doesn't pollute the scorecard. The only references are the hardcoded structural value and the policy-driven routing minimum, so there's no invented production constants to worry about.\n\nNow I'm thinking through potential blocking issues, particularly around concurrency correctness under READ COMMITTED isolation...\n\nThe advisory lock serialization combined with the partial unique index provides the correctness guarantee. Even though the hash function could theoretically collide, that would only cause unnecessary serialization, not actual incorrectness. The unique index is what actually prevents duplicate accepted rows. And the ORDER BY LIMIT 1 is redundant since the partial unique index ensures at most one accepted row exists anyway. If two settlements race and both try to insert accepted=true, the advisory lock blocks the second one until the first commits, and then the unique index rejects any duplicate attempt.\n\nDR-089 is satisfied: the settlement checks TERMINAL status before writing, runs outside the lifecycle in the scheduler with separate credentials, and the watch enforces that outcomes require a registered policy. The append-only constraint is maintained through reject_mutation triggers on all relevant tables, with settlement only inserting new rows and never updating the winner. One thing I'm noticing though: the scheduler CLI runs with no outcomes and no policy, so the published job effectively does nothing when actually executed.\n\nThe resolver-outcome adapter that would supply those outcomes isn't wired yet—the watch is a published seam waiting for the resolver adapter to be integrated. This is framed explicitly in the code as an incomplete handoff, but it's within scope as the standing watch seam itself; the resolver integration is a separate concern. So this is a non-blocking carry-forward, not a defect in what's being claimed here.\n\nNow I'm reconsidering whether this is actually blocking. First accepted → `accepted=true`, `superseded_by=null`; a later competing settlement → `accepted=false` with `superseded_by_answer_outcome_id` pointing at the winner, appended with its own `SETTLEMENT_ATTEMPT_SUPERSEDED` + read-back. The winner row is never rewritten; the loser is never deleted. A partial unique index `answer_outcome_first_settled_wins ... WHERE accepted` (migrations/0015_s12.sql:58) is a DB-level backstop.
